尼日利亚采取行动,通过“同一个健康”方法预防 狂犬病 死亡

Nigeria steps up action to prevent rabies deaths through One Health approach
新闻简报尼日利亚正在加紧努力,通过整合人类、动物和环境卫生部门的“同一个健康”战略来预防 狂犬病 死亡。正如在阿布贾举行的 2026 年世界 狂犬病 日纪念活动中强调的那样,联邦政府承诺到 2030 年终止由狗介导的 狂犬病 造成的人类死亡。2025 年 11 月的一次全国研讨会审查了 狂犬病 快速响应工具包,该工具包指导 狂犬病、调查和对可疑 狂犬病 事件的响应。该工具包强调了动物咬伤后立即清洁伤口和及时医疗护理的重要性。2026 年 1 月至 8 月期间,16 个州记录了超过 100 起动物 狂犬病 病例。联邦畜牧业发展部领导狗 疫苗接种 和动物 监测,而联邦卫生和社会福利部则重点关注人类暴露监测和 治疗 的获取。联邦环境部支持公众宣传活动,以促进与动物更安全的互动。世卫组织提供技术支持,以加强 狂犬病 预防、狂犬病 和应对。该倡议旨在通过政府、卫生专业人员、社区和狗主人之间的持续合作来减少 狂犬病 传播。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该新闻报道于 2026 年 10 月 7 日发布。世界 狂犬病 日纪念活动于 2026 年 9 月 28 日举行。国家研讨会于 2025 年 11 月举行。
地点与对象
尼日利亚,特别是阿布贾和 16 个州。重点是社区、家庭和狗主人。
事件
尼日利亚正在通过“同一个健康”方法加强 狂犬病 预防,该方法涉及人类、动物和环境卫生部门之间的合作。联邦政府重申其承诺,到 2030 年终止由狗介导的 狂犬病 造成的人类死亡。
规模、影响与风险
2026 年 1 月至 8 月期间,16 个州记录了 100 多例动物 狂犬病 病例。一旦出现临床症状,狂犬病 就会致命,但它是可以预防的。目标是到 2030 年结束由狗介导的 狂犬病 造成的人类死亡。
应对、状态与不确定性
联邦畜牧业发展部领导动物 监测、狗 疫苗接种 和兽医服务。联邦卫生和社会福利部支持人类接触 监测 和获得护理。联邦环境部支持公众意识以及人与动物之间更安全的互动。世卫组织提供有关 监测、风险评估和应对协调的技术指导。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Nigeria steps up action to prevent rabies deaths through One Health approach

News Brief
Nigeria is intensifying efforts to prevent rabies deaths through a One Health strategy that integrates human, animal, and environmental health sectors. The Federal Government has committed to ending human deaths from dog-mediated rabies by 2030, as highlighted during the 2026 World Rabies Day commemoration in Abuja. A national workshop in November 2025 reviewed the Rabies Rapid Response Toolkit, which guides surveillance, investigation, and response to suspected rabies events. The toolkit emphasizes the importance of immediate wound cleaning and prompt medical care after animal bites. Over 100 animal rabies cases were recorded in 16 states between January and August 2026. The Federal Ministry of Livestock Development leads dog vaccination and animal surveillance, while the Federal Ministry of Health and Social Welfare focuses on human exposure monitoring and access to treatment. The Federal Ministry of Environment supports public awareness campaigns to promote safer interactions with animals. WHO provides technical support to strengthen rabies prevention, surveillance, and response. The initiative aims to reduce rabies transmission through sustained collaboration between government, health professionals, communities, and dog owners.
Original source text (English)

Work Building a Healthier Africa, Together: A manifesto for transformation WHO Africa / Countries / Nigeria / Nigeria steps up action to prevent rabies deaths through One Health approach Nigeria steps up action to prevent rabies deaths through One Health approach 7 October 2026 | Feature Story | Reading time: 4 min Nigeria steps up action to prevent rabies deaths through One Health approach More pictures More images Abuja, Nigeria is strengthening dog vaccination, surveillance and public awareness to prevent rabies deaths and advance efforts to end human deaths from dog-mediated rabies by 2030. The 2026 World Rabies Day commemoration on 28 September brought together government institutions and partners under the theme “Stronger Together”, with a call for coordinated action across the human, animal and environmental health sectors. Rabies is fatal once clinical symptoms appear, but it is preventable. Vaccinating dogs, preventing bites and ensuring people receive prompt treatment after exposure can save lives. Rabies awareness activities are helping people understand the disease and how to prevent it. During a sensitization campaign, Daniel Mshelia, a security guard who works around dogs, learnt about the risks of rabies, the importance of dog vaccination and the need to seek care immediately after an animal bite. “Before then, I knew little about rabies or the danger an infected animal could pose. I learnt that a dog bite could lead to death if treatment is not sought early,” he recalled. His experience shows the importance of ensuring people know how to prevent rabies and what to do after possible exposure. Preventing rabies at its source At the World Rabies Day commemoration in Abuja, the Federal Government reaffirmed its commitment to ending human deaths from dog-mediated rabies by 2030. “Protecting communities from rabies requires everyone to play a part. We must stop working in silos and continue to educate families, encourage responsible animal ownership, and work together to reduce risks in our communities,” said Dr Chinyere Ijeoma Akujobi, Permanent Secretary, Federal Ministry of Livestock Development. According to the Federal Ministry of Livestock Development, more than 100 animal rabies cases were recorded between January and August 2026 across 16 states. Nigeria is strengthening rabies prevention through a One Health approach that brings together the human, animal and environmental health sectors, as the country works towards the global goal of ending human deaths from dog-mediated rabies by 2030. The Federal Ministry of Livestock Development leads animal surveillance, dog vaccination and veterinary services. The Federal Ministry of Health and Social Welfare supports surveillance of human exposures and access to care, while the Federal Ministry of Environment supports public awareness and safer interaction between people and animals. Mrs Dorcas Mernyi, Assistant Director, Rabies Programme Manager and One Health Desk Officer at the Federal Ministry of Health and Social Welfare, called for prompt care following exposure. “No one should die from rabies simply because they did not know what to do after a dog bite or could not access treatment. We must ensure health workers recognise possible exposure immediately and that patients receive appropriate care without delay. Strengthening surveillance and response In November 2025, the Federal Ministry of Livestock Development, with support from the World Health Organization (WHO) and other One Health partners, convened a national workshop to review, test and validate the Rabies Rapid Response Toolkit. The toolkit guides surveillance, investigation and response to suspected rabies events. It helps teams identify people who may have been exposed, improve reporting and coordinate action between public health and veterinary services. WHO also supports government-led rabies investigations through technical guidance on surveillance, risk assessment and response coordination. Dr Alexander Chimbaru, WHO Emergency Preparedness and Response Cluster Lead in Nigeria, said preventing deaths requires action throughout the year. “Ending human deaths from dog-mediated rabies requires sustained collaboration between government, health and veterinary professionals, communities and dog owners. Every dog vaccinated, every exposure reported early and every person who receives timely care takes us closer to the 2030 goal.” Acting quickly can save lives The NCDC one health toolkit advises that anyone bitten or scratched by an animal suspected of having rabies should immediately wash the wound thoroughly with soap and running water for at least 15 minutes and seek medical care without delay. Depending on the exposure, PEP may include rabies vaccine and rabies immunoglobulin or monoclonal antibodies. People should not wait for symptoms before seeking care. Preventing infection in dogs is equally important. WHO recommends sustained vaccination coverage of at least 70% of dogs in at-risk areas to interrupt transmission. WHO will continue to support the Government of Nigeria and partners to strengthen rabies prevention, surveillance and response towards ending human deaths from dog-mediated rabies by 2030. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Guilford County confirms 14th case of animal rabies in 2026 - FOX8 WGHP

News Brief
Guilford County, North Carolina, has confirmed the 14th case of animal rabies in 2026, involving a raccoon found on Kearn Street in Jamestown. The confirmation was made by the Guilford County Health and Human Services Agency, Department of Public Health, on Thursday, October 7, 2026, and the report was published on October 8, 2026. The agency is alerting residents about the rabies case and advising them to supervise pets outdoors, avoid contact with wildlife, and contact Animal Control for more information. Rabies circulates within the local wildlife population throughout the year, indicating a potential risk to humans and pets. The response includes ongoing investigation and public awareness efforts to prevent further exposure. Residents are encouraged to report any stray animals or sick wildlife to local authorities.

FOX8 WGHP Guilford County confirms 14th case of animal rabies in 2026 Jim Sands 2 days ago GUILFORD COUNTY, N.C. (WGHP) — The Guilford County Health and Human Services Agency, Department of Public Health, is alerting residents that another case of animal rabies has been reported. The agency discovered a raccoon on Kearn Street in Jamestown that tested positive for the rabies virus on Thursday. This is the 14th confirmed case of animal rabies in 2026. Rabies circulates within the local wildlife population throughout the year. Animals that are confined in outdoor fenced areas should also have current rabies vaccinations. Supervising pets when they are outdoors could prevent contact with wild animals and lessen the possibility of rabies exposure. Tips to help prevent you or your family from being exposed to rabies Avoid direct contact with wildlife, dead or alive. Do not approach, try to play with, touch, rescue or treat any wildlife. If you find a sick or injured animal, contact Animal Control at 336-641-5990. Avoid any animal displaying unnatural behavior. Wild animals are not usually friendly so be very careful if they approach you. Do not try to separate fighting animals. Feed your pets indoors. Leaving food outside will attract dogs, cats and other wildlife into your yard. Report all stray animals to Animal Control at 336-641-5990 in Greensboro and 336-883-3224 in High Point. Stray animals may not have current rabies vaccinations. If a person is bitten by an animal (domestic or stray), wash the bitten area immediately with soap and water, seek medical attention and report the bite to local animal control officers. 降压药地尔硫卓可阻止 狂犬病病毒 进入并拯救受感染的小鼠

Blood Pressure Drug Diltiazem Blocks Rabies Virus Entry and Saves Infected Mice
新闻简报2026 年 10 月 10 日发表在 PLOS Pathogens 上的一项研究表明,地尔硫卓(一种常见的降压药物)可以阻止 狂犬病病毒 进入细胞并拯救受感染的小鼠。由哈尔滨兽医研究所科学家领导的这项研究发现,狂犬病病毒 使用钾通道(KCa1.1)和钙通道(Cav1.2)进入细胞。通过用地尔硫卓或帕西林抑制这些通道,病毒的内化显着减少。在实验中,70% 接受地尔硫卓治疗的小鼠在致命的 狂犬病 感染中幸存,而所有对照小鼠均死亡。研究结果表明,地尔硫卓可以作为现有 狂犬病 治疗 的经济替代品或补充品,特别是在免疫球蛋白获取有限的地区。世界卫生组织估计,每年有 59,000 人死于 狂犬病,其中近一半是 15 岁以下的儿童。该研究强调了重新利用 狂犬病 治疗 现有药物的潜力,为改善全球医疗服务的可及性带来了希望。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于 2026 年 10 月 10 日发表。实验进行的时间包括在 4°C 和 37°C 下孵育,并在感染后 24 小时和 48 小时观察结果。这些小鼠接受了 9 至 16 天的接种和治疗。
地点与对象
该研究在中国农业科学院哈尔滨兽医研究所进行。该研究涉及小鼠神经母细胞瘤 N2a 细胞、人 HEK293 细胞和人 SK-N-SH 神经母细胞瘤细胞。使用的小鼠是六周龄的 C57BL/6J 小鼠。
事件
Researchers discovered that diltiazem, a blood pressure medication, blocks the entry of rabies virus into cells and rescued approximately 70 percent of infected mice. The study involved identifying host factors required for rabies virus replication, testing the effects of diltiazem and paxilline on viral entry, and confirming the mechanism through various assays.
规模、影响与风险
研究表明,地尔硫卓显着降低了细胞培养物中 狂犬病病毒 的内化,并拯救了 70% 的感染小鼠。世界卫生组织估计,每年有 59,000 人死于 狂犬病,其中近一半是 15 岁以下的儿童。该研究强调了地尔硫卓作为 狂犬病 负担得起的 治疗 选项的潜力,特别是在免疫球蛋白获取有限的地区。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究发表在 PLOS Pathogens 上,并通过多种实验方法得到证实,包括 siRNA 敲低、化学抑制剂和显微镜检测。这些发现基于体外和体内实验,并通过免疫共沉淀和肌动蛋白聚合测定验证了作用机制。该研究正在进行中,作者提出地尔硫卓作为现有 暴露后预防 的潜在治疗补充。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Blood Pressure Drug Diltiazem Blocks Rabies Virus Entry and Saves Infected Mice

News Brief
A study published on October 10, 2026, in PLOS Pathogens reveals that diltiazem, a common blood pressure medication, can block the entry of rabies virus into cells and save infected mice. The research, led by scientists at the Harbin Veterinary Research Institute, identified that the rabies virus uses a potassium channel (KCa1.1) and a calcium channel (Cav1.2) to enter cells. By inhibiting these channels with diltiazem or paxilline, the virus's internalization was significantly reduced. In experiments, 70% of mice treated with diltiazem survived a lethal rabies infection, while all control mice died. The findings suggest that diltiazem could serve as an affordable alternative or supplement to existing rabies treatments, especially in regions with limited access to immunoglobulins. The World Health Organization estimates that 59,000 people die from rabies annually, with nearly half being children under fifteen. The study highlights the potential of repurposing existing drugs for rabies treatment, offering hope for improving global access to care.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Saturday, October 10, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Biology Blood Pressure Drug Diltiazem Blocks Rabies Virus Entry and Saves Infected Mice by October 10, 2026 in Biology, Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Blood Pressure Drug Diltiazem Blocks Rabies Virus Entry and Saves Infected Mice Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Rabies remains one of the deadliest infectious diseases known to medicine. Once the virus reaches the central nervous system and clinical symptoms appear, mortality approaches 100 percent, and the World Health Organization estimates that roughly 59,000 people die of the disease each year, with nearly half of the victims children under the age of fifteen. Although effective vaccines and rabies immunoglobulins exist, their cost and chronic global supply shortages leave many people in rural Asia and Africa dangerously unprotected after exposure to a rabid animal. A new study published in PLOS Pathogens now offers a strikingly practical way forward: researchers report that diltiazem, a cheap and widely used FDA-approved blood pressure medication, blocks the entry of rabies virus into cells and rescued approximately 70 percent of mice from a lethal infection. The research, led by Ziruo Sun, Jinliang Wang, Zhigao Bu and colleagues at the Harbin Veterinary Research Institute of the Chinese Academy of Agricultural Sciences, began with a systematic search for the host machinery that rabies virus depends on to invade cells. In a previous genome-wide screen, the team had used 64,755 small interfering RNAs targeting 21,585 human genes in HEK293 cells to identify host factors required for rabies virus replication. Among the hits were sixteen genes related to potassium channels, and the strongest effect came from silencing KCNMA1, the gene that encodes the pore-forming subunit of the large conductance calcium- and voltage-activated potassium channel known as KCa1.1, a seven-transmembrane protein sitting in the plasma membrane. To confirm that this channel genuinely matters for infection, the researchers knocked down KCa1.1 expression in both mouse neuroblastoma N2a cells and human HEK293 cells using specific siRNAs. Quantitative PCR verified that the channel’s mRNA was substantially reduced, and when the silenced cells were infected with a recombinant rabies virus strain expressing enhanced green fluorescent protein, the amount of infectious virus released into the culture supernatant dropped significantly at both 24 and 48 hours after infection. The team then turned to paxilline, a selective chemical inhibitor of KCa1.1 that binds the inner pore of the channel and blocks potassium conductance. In N2a, HEK293, and human SK-N-SH neuroblastoma cells, paxilline had no effect on cell viability but suppressed rabies virus replication in a clear dose-dependent manner, and time-course experiments confirmed the inhibition persisted across the tested period. Because KCa1.1 is expressed primarily at the plasma membrane, the investigators hypothesized that it might act during viral entry rather than during later stages of the viral life cycle. Entry involves two separable steps: attachment of virus particles to the cell surface and their subsequent internalization. By incubating cells with virus at 4 degrees Celsius, a temperature that permits binding but prevents internalization, and then quantifying attached viral RNA, the team showed that neither KCa1.1 silencing nor paxilline treatment changed how much virus bound to cells. When the temperature was shifted to 37 degrees to allow entry, however, and surface-bound virus was stripped away with trypsin before analysis, the amount of viral RNA inside treated or silenced cells fell dramatically. A complementary microscopy assay, which labeled viral glycoprotein under conditions that distinguish particles remaining on the surface from those that had entered, showed that paxilline-treated cells accumulated more virus on their surface while their total particle count stayed the same. Together, these experiments demonstrated that activated KCa1.1 is specifically required for the internalization step of rabies virus entry. A co-immunoprecipitation assay revealed that KCa1.1 does not physically interact with the rabies virus glycoprotein, the viral surface protein that drives attachment and clathrin-mediated endocytosis. That result pointed to an intermediary. Prior work had shown that KCa1.1 assembles into macromolecular complexes with voltage-gated calcium channels, including Cav1.2, and that calcium influx through these channels can activate KCa1.1. Notably, Cav1.2 was the only calcium channel that emerged from the team’s earlier genome-wide screen. Co-immunoprecipitation confirmed a specific interaction between Cav1.2 and KCa1.1, and silencing Cav1.2 in N2a and HEK293 cells significantly reduced rabies virus titers, establishing that the L-type calcium channel is also required for productive infection. The mechanistic picture became sharper when the researchers examined the role of the viral glycoprotein itself. Co-immunoprecipitation showed that Cav1.2 specifically interacts with the rabies virus glycoprotein, and a pull-down assay using purified virus particles demonstrated that Cav1.2 captures rabies virions in a dose-dependent manner. Binding assays revealed that Cav1.2 silencing or blockade did not reduce how much virus attached to cells, but internalization assays showed far less viral RNA inside treated cells. In other words, the glycoprotein engages Cav1.2, and this engagement is required to activate the channel and trigger the internalization machinery, even though the virus still docks normally on the cell surface when the channel is disabled. The team also identified the downstream cellular process that the channel axis manipulates. Rabies virus enters cells through clathrin-mediated endocytosis with the help of actin, and previous work had shown that KCa1.1 promotes influenza virus entry by regulating F-actin polymerization. Using phalloidin staining to visualize filamentous actin, the researchers found that rabies virus entry induces F-actin polymerization in HEK293 cells, and that this polymerization was markedly reduced when Cav1.2 or KCa1.1 were knocked down or when cells were treated with diltiazem or paxilline. The authors therefore propose a sequential model: rabies virus glycoprotein binds Cav1.2 and triggers its activation; activated Cav1.2 then interacts with and activates KCa1.1; and activated KCa1.1 drives F-actin polymerization that powers the internalization of the virus particle. The therapeutic implications rest on diltiazem, a benzothiazepine-class L-type calcium channel blocker approved in the United States in 1982 and long prescribed for hypertension, angina, and atrial arrhythmias. In cell culture, diltiazem blocked rabies virus infection in a dose-dependent manner without affecting viability, and four other FDA-approved Cav1.2 antagonists, the phenylalkylamine verapamil and the dihydropyridines nifedipine, nicardipine, and nimodipine, also inhibited the virus in vitro. The decisive test came in mice. Six-week-old C57BL/6J mice were inoculated intramuscularly with ten 50 percent mouse lethal doses of the rabies street virus GX/09, and six hours later they began receiving daily intramuscular injections of diltiazem at 25 or 50 milligrams per kilogram, or a phosphate buffer control. All 19 control mice died within 9 to 16 days with typical rabies signs such as body tremors and hind limb paralysis. At the lower dose, 5 of 19 mice survived, roughly 26 percent, while at 50 milligrams per kilogram, 13 of 19 mice survived, approximately 70 percent, and the survivors remained completely asymptomatic throughout a 28-day observation period. Measurements of viral burden supported the idea that diltiazem works by blocking entry before the virus reaches the brain. No viral RNA was detectable in the brains of treated or control mice at day 4, but by day 6, viral RNA was present in all six control animals examined and in only two of six diltiazem-treated animals, and immunohistochemistry at day 8 detected rabies antigen in all control brains but only one of three treated brains. When the researchers delayed diltiazem administration until days 6 and 7, after viral replication was already underway in the brain, they observed no significant reduction in brain viral RNA, indicating that the drug’s protection is unlikely to stem from direct suppression of established central nervous system infection. Notably, the 50 milligram per kilogram mouse dose corresponds, after body surface area normalization under FDA guidelines, to a human equivalent dose of roughly 4 milligrams per kilogram, or about 240 milligrams per day for a 60-kilogram adult, which falls squarely within the range already used clinically for cardiovascular indications. The authors argue that diltiazem could serve as an affordable, mass-produced complement to existing post-exposure prophylaxis, offering a stopgap where rabies immunoglobulin is unavailable and a potential enhancement where it is not, since it is estimated that only 1 to 10 percent of category III exposure patients currently receive the recommended treatment. The findings also extend a growing body of evidence that ion channels are conserved entry factors for diverse viruses: Cav1.2 has been implicated in SARS-CoV-2 and influenza virus entry, KCa1.1 in influenza virus endocytosis, and diltiazem has shown activity against rotavirus, porcine reproductive and respiratory syndrome virus, and porcine deltacoronavirus. With no approved drugs currently targeting KCa1.1, the potassium channel remains a target for future drug development, while Cav1.2, already served by three structural classes of clinically validated antagonists, stands out as a promising foundation for broad-spectrum antiviral strategies. Further studies will be needed to evaluate the in vivo efficacy of the other calcium channel blockers and to determine whether diltiazem concentrations in the central nervous system reach antiviral levels, but the prospect of repurposing a decades-old cardiovascular pill against one of humanity’s most lethal pathogens gives the global effort to eliminate rabies deaths a genuinely new weapon to test. Penang stays on alert for human rabies after Kedah man dies of infection - Malay Mail
新闻简报2026 年 9 月 27 日,一名来自吉打州的 31 岁男子在 8 月份被流浪狗咬伤后死于感染,马来西亚槟城一直对人类 狂犬病 保持警惕。这是吉打州自 1998 年以来首例人类 狂犬病 病例。槟城自 1975 年以来就没有报告过任何人类 狂犬病 病例,该州也没有发现任何动物 狂犬病 病例。包括丹尼尔·古伊·兹森在内的州卫生部门官员已证实没有新病例,但强调为潜在事件做好准备。他们正在与兽医服务部合作监测情况,并建议公众遵循卫生部关于动物咬伤的指导方针,例如立即就医。应对措施仍在继续,当局保持警惕并确保遵守协议以防止进一步传播。

时间
The news report was published on 2026-10-05. The event date is September 27, 2026, when a 31-year-old man from Kedah died of rabies. The situation in Penang is ongoing as of the publication date.

该活动涉及马来西亚槟城和吉打州。受影响的人群包括一名来自吉打古邦巴苏(Kubang Pasu)、死于 狂犬病 的 31 岁男子,以及建议遵守健康指南的槟城公众。

吉打州一名 31 岁男子于 2026 年 8 月被流浪狗咬伤后死于 狂犬病。槟城自 1975 年以来没有报告任何人类 狂犬病 病例,迄今为止槟城也没有发现动物 狂犬病 病例。州卫生部和兽医服务部正在监测情况。
规模、影响与风险
吉打州报告了一例人类 狂犬病 病例,这是自 1998 年以来的第一例。槟城自 1975 年以来没有任何人类 狂犬病 病例,迄今为止也没有动物病例。风险没有明确说明,但当局已准备好应对潜在事件。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Penang stays on alert for human rabies after Kedah man dies of infection - Malay Mail

News Brief
Penang, Malaysia, has remained on alert for human rabies following the death of a 31-year-old man from Kedah on September 27, 2026, who succumbed to the infection after being bitten by a stray dog in August. This marks the first human rabies case in Kedah since 1998. Penang has not reported any human rabies cases since 1975, and no animal rabies cases have been detected in the state. State Health Department officials, including Daniel Gooi Zi Sen, have confirmed the absence of new cases but emphasized preparedness for potential incidents. They are collaborating with the Department of Veterinary Services to monitor the situation and advise the public to follow Ministry of Health guidelines for animal bites, such as seeking immediate medical attention. The response is ongoing, with authorities maintaining vigilance and ensuring adherence to protocols to prevent further spread.

First Published: Monday, 05 Oct 2026 5:17 PM MYT × Subscribe to Newsletter Subscribe GEORGE TOWN, Oct 5 — Penang has recorded no human rabies cases since the last two cases were reported in 1975, with the Penang State Health Department remaining on standby to respond to any possible cases. State Youth, Sports and Health Committee chairman Daniel Gooi Zi Sen said no animal rabies cases had also been detected in the state to date. “No new cases have been reported thus far, but we are always prepared to deal with any incidents,” he said at a press conference for the Penang state-level Clean, Smoke-Free Premises Programme at Food Bayana Medan here today. Gooi said the state government was also working with the Department of Veterinary Services to monitor the rabies situation in Penang. He advised the public to follow Ministry of Health guidelines if bitten by an animal, including seeking immediate medical attention at any healthcare facility to ensure those exposed to infection risks receive proper examination and appropriate treatment. On Sept 27, a 31-year-old man from Kubang Pasu, Kedah, died of rabies after reportedly being bitten by a stray dog in August, marking the first human rabies case reported in the state since 1998. — Bernama You May Also Like Related Articles Malaysia / 6 m ago Malaysia has spent decades digitising public healthcare. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

美国农业部计划本周在北卡罗来纳州西部投放 狂犬病 疫苗 诱饵 - WLOS

Rabies vaccine bait drops planned this week in western North Carolina by USDA - WLOS
新闻简报The USDA is planning rabies vaccine bait drops in western North Carolina this week as part of an Oral Rabies Vaccination (ORV) program to prevent the westward spread of the rabies virus. The program will involve ground teams distributing baits from October 5-9 in Asheville city limits, helicopter baiting in urban and suburban areas from October 9-13, and low-flying airplanes dropping baits in rural counties from October 15-22. Over 2.4 million baits will be distributed across western North Carolina, southwest Virginia, east Tennessee, northcentral Alabama, and north Georgia, with more than 600,000 in North Carolina alone. The ORV program aims to create a barrier along the Appalachian Mountains to stop the spread of raccoon rabies. The USDA emphasized that rabies is a serious public health concern, and untreated exposures are almost always fatal. The baiting efforts are expected to be completed by late October, depending on weather and other factors. The program is conducted in coordination with the Animal and Plant Health Inspection Service, Wildlife Services, and the North Carolina Department of Health and Human Services. The vaccine baits are safe for many species, including domestic dogs and cats, and humans and pets are advised not to make contact with or feed wildlife and to keep their pets’ rabies vaccinations current.
查看新闻五要素
时间
狂犬病 疫苗诱饵投放计划将在本周进行,北卡罗来纳州西部的地面团队将在10月5日至9日期间投放诱饵,直升机投放将在10月9日至13日期间进行,低空飞行的飞机将在10月15日至22日期间在农村地区投放诱饵。该计划预计将在10月下旬完成,具体时间取决于天气和其他因素。(N7,N8,N9,N10,N11,N17).
地点与对象
诱饵投放将在北卡罗来纳州西部进行,特别是在大阿什维尔、伯恩斯维尔、马尔斯山和韦恩斯维尔的山区、城市和郊区社区,以及阿什、艾弗里、邦库姆、切罗基、克莱、格雷厄姆、海伍德、亨德森、杰克逊、梅肯、麦迪逊、麦克道尔、米切尔、斯温、特兰西瓦尼亚、沃托加和扬西县的农村地区。
事件
美国农业部计划在北卡罗来纳州西部进行狂犬病 疫苗诱饵投放,作为一项口服狂犬病 疫苗接种(ORV)计划,以防止狂犬病病毒向西传播。该计划由动物与植物健康检验服务、野生动物服务以及北卡罗来纳州卫生与人类服务部协调进行。(N7,N8,N11,N12,N16).
规模、影响与风险
The ORV program will distribute over 2.4 million baits across western North Carolina, southwest Virginia, east Tennessee, northcentral Alabama, and north Georgia, with over 600,000 baits in North Carolina alone. The USDA emphasized that rabies is a serious public health concern and that untreated exposures are almost always fatal.
应对、状态与不确定性
美国农业部正在进行投放诱饵活动,作为防止浣熊 狂犬病 传播的更大努力的一部分。该计划在多个机场地点开展,将于 10 月份持续约三周。根据天气和其他因素,预计诱饵工作将于 10 月下旬完成。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Rabies vaccine bait drops planned this week in western North Carolina by USDA - WLOS

News Brief
The USDA is planning rabies vaccine bait drops in western North Carolina this week as part of an Oral Rabies Vaccination (ORV) program to prevent the westward spread of the rabies virus. The program will involve ground teams distributing baits from October 5-9 in Asheville city limits, helicopter baiting in urban and suburban areas from October 9-13, and low-flying airplanes dropping baits in rural counties from October 15-22. Over 2.4 million baits will be distributed across western North Carolina, southwest Virginia, east Tennessee, northcentral Alabama, and north Georgia, with more than 600,000 in North Carolina alone. Why is the ORV program conducted in Appalachians? topics: Rabies USDA Oral rabies vaccination Wildlife Services North Carolina Asheville Helicopter baiting Raccoons WESTERN NORTH CAROLINA (WLOS) — Oral Rabies Vaccination (ORV) bait drops are planned for this week in the mountains of western North Carolina. According to a news release from the United States Department of Agriculture, the drops will be made in the area on the following days: Oct. 5-9: Ground teams will distribute baits by vehicle in the Asheville city limits. Oct. 9-13: Helicopter baiting will be conducted in the urban and suburban communities in greater Asheville, Burnsville, Mars Hill and Waynesville Oct. 15-22: Low-flying airplanes will be dropping baits in the rural areas of the following counties: Ashe, Avery, Buncombe, Cherokee, Clay, Graham, Haywood, Henderson, Jackson, Macon, Madison, McDowell, Mitchell, Swain, Transylvania, Watauga, and Yancey The USDA said it is conducting this drops in coordination with Animal and Plant Health Inspection Service, Wildlife Services and the North Carolina Department of Health and Human Services. The USDA said in the release that the smell of the ORV baits attracts targeted wild animals, such as raccoons, who eat the baits and are then vaccinated against rabies. HELENE DEBRIS 'STILL A FACTOR' IN NC WILDFIRE RISK, OFFICIAL SAYS Powered By 10 Sec Pensacola home catches fire before Isaias nears peak; officer rescues trapped man Next Stay The ORV bait distribution program is part of management activities to prevent the westward movement of the rabies virus most often spread by raccoons, the release said. ORV baits are distributed using small airplanes and helicopters from the air and vehicles on the ground. The project is based out of multiple airport locations and will take place for about three weeks in October. During the baiting program more than 2.4 million baits will be distributed in areas of western North Carolina, southwest Virginia, east Tennessee, northcentral Alabama and north Georgia, including over 600,000 baits in North Carolina, the release said. All baiting efforts should be completed by late October, depending on weather and other factors. Rabies is caused by a virus that infects the central nervous system in mammals and represents a serious public health concern. If exposures to the virus are not treated it is almost always fatal, the USDA said. Costs associated with detection, prevention and control of rabies exceed $600 million annually in the U.S. According to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, about 90 percent of reported rabies cases in the U.S. are in wildlife. CAMP LIKE IT’S THE 1900S: CRADLE OF FORESTRY HOSTS OLD-STYLE CAMPING EVENT People are urged not to make contact with or feed wildlife and to keep their pets’ rabies vaccinations current. The USDA said in the release that the vaccine baits have been proven safe in many species of animals, including domestic dogs and cats. Humans and pets cannot get rabies from contact with the baits but are asked to leave them undisturbed should they encounter them. If contact with baits occurs, immediately rinse the contact area with warm water and soap. According to the release, ORV baits have been distributed in North Carolina since 2005 as part of a larger effort by the Wildlife Services, National Rabies Management Program to prevent the westward spread of raccoon rabies by creating a barrier along the Appalachian Mountains from the Canadian border to Alabama. The program is conducted in coordination with the Animal and Plant Health Inspection Service, Wildlife Services, and the North Carolina Department of Health and Human Services. Scale, impact and risk
The ORV program will distribute over 2.4 million baits across western North Carolina, southwest Virginia, east Tennessee, northcentral Alabama, and north Georgia, with over 600,000 baits in North Carolina alone. The USDA emphasized that rabies is a serious public health concern and that untreated exposures are almost always fatal.
Response, status and uncertainty

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Bat-human interactions are on the rise, leading to rabies concerns - Ideastream Public Media

News Brief
The article reports an increase in bat-human interactions in Cuyahoga County, Ohio, leading to more rabies testing. This year, five bats have tested positive for rabies, double the usual number, with a total of 107 bats tested so far, compared to about 50 in a typical year. The positivity rate remains within the normal range of 3.6%. Experts speculate that the rise in interactions may be due to bat population recovery after past disease impacts, though this is not confirmed. The CDC has noted a nationwide increase in reported human rabies exposures, but no human cases have been recorded in Ohio since 1970, largely due to effective post-exposure prophylaxis treatments. Authorities recommend contacting local health departments if exposed to bats to assess risk and receive appropriate medical care.

Public Media Careers Internships Volunteer Ways to Donate Sponsor Our Programs Newsletters Search Health Bat-human interactions are on the rise, leading to rabies concerns Ideastream Public Media | By Ida Lieszkovszky Published October 5, 2026 at 5:45 AM EDT Facebook Twitter LinkedIn Email Listen • 1:00 Ivan Kuzmin / Adobe Stock Images Experts recommend calling your local health department if you have been exposed to bats, such as these big brown bats that have moved into an attic. There has been an uptick in the number of bats testing positive for rabies both locally and nationwide, though experts caution that doesn’t mean rabies rates are on the rise. So far this year, five bats have tested positive for rabies in Cuyahoga County, double the normal amount. “That was unusual because typically in Cuyahoga County, we see maybe one or two positive bats for an entire year,” said Stacey Koltas, an environmental health program manager at the Cuyahoga County Board of Health. A total of 107 bats have been sent for testing so far this year from Cuyahoga County, compared with about 50 in a typical year. Koltas said that shows the number of bat-human interactions are up, because those interactions are what trigger testing. “When you increase the denominator like that, you dilute the rate, and so what we're seeing still is an infectivity rate that's within what we expect to see, both at the county level and at the state level,” Koltas said. The reason for the rise in human encounters with bats isn't yet fully understood, she said. State and federal tracking has seen the same trend. “We’ve already tested more animals this year than we usually do for an entire 12-month period," Ohio State Public Health Veterinarian Sara Margrey said in an emailed statement. The increase includes a 143% increase in bat submissions compared to 2022-2025, Margrey said, though the positivity rate is still within the normal range of about 3.6% of bats tested. The Centers for Disease Control sent out an advisory last month warning of a “nationwide increase in reported human rabies exposures.” There has not been a human case of rabies in Ohio since 1970 — largely because of treatment (including the rabies vaccine) given to people who've been exposed to rabid animals. In the U.S. overall, about 100,000 people each year receive that treatment after encountering rabid animals. If you do come into contact with a bat, or wake to find one in your home, Koltas recommended calling your local health department to be evaluated. “We can either send the bat to our state lab for rabies testing, to rule out the risk for rabies, or advise the individuals who were exposed to receive the post-exposure prophylaxis,” she said. Why encounters are on the rise While the reason for the uptick in bat-human interactions is unknown, Koltas speculated that it could be because bat populations are finally starting to rebound, after having been decimated by disease over the past several years. According to the Ohio Department of Natural Resources, bat numbers are stabilizing in some species but not necessarily increasing. University of Cincinnati Associate Professor Joseph Johnson, the principal investigator of The Ohio Bat Lab, said in an e-mail that “bat populations are hard to quantify.” He also noted that it’s difficult to track rabies levels in bats because they need to be killed, or already dead, for their brains to be tested for the disease. They are also a protected species, making random testing impossible. “This means... the tests we get come from encounters with sick animals, which is a little bit of a sampling bias and needs to be interpreted with a grain of salt,” he said. Johnson noted that it’s never a good idea to touch or pick up wildlife. It’s not just bats that can spread rabies. In Ohio, raccoons, skunks, coyotes and foxes can also spread the disease and pets are also capable of being infected. But humans tend to have more interactions with bats than with other species because bats tend to take refuge in warm places such as houses when winter is on the horizon. Event
An increase in bat-human interactions has been reported, with experts recommending contacting local health departments for evaluation and rabies testing.
Scale, impact and risk
Five bats have tested positive for rabies in Cuyahoga County this year, double the normal amount. A total of 107 bats have been tested so far, compared to about 50 in a typical year. The positivity rate remains within the normal range of about 3.6%. There has not been a human case of rabies in Ohio since 1970, largely due to post-exposure prophylaxis treatments.

USDA to airdrop rabies vaccine baits across Northwest Georgia in effort to stop virus's spread - CBS News
新闻简报美国农业部计划在乔治亚州西北部空投超过 500,000 个 狂犬病 疫苗 诱饵,目标是浣熊和其他野生动物,作为阻止病毒传播的年度努力的一部分。该行动从 10 月份开始,涉及直升机和飞机空投,旨在减少与 狂犬病 相关的人类健康和安全威胁,并最终消除美国的地面 狂犬病。官员建议公众不要触摸或移动这些诱饵,这些诱饵涂有鱼粉,并贴有免费举报电话号码。居民应该在空投期间看到低空飞行的飞机,如果接触到 疫苗,请洗手。该计划是美国农业部、疾病预防控制中心和当地机构之间的合作项目。

空投行动计划在接下来的几周内开始,直升机空投将在10月左右开始,飞机空投则在同一时间左右(N7,N8)。发表日期为2026-10-07(N1)。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

USDA to airdrop rabies vaccine baits across Northwest Georgia in effort to stop virus's spread - CBS News

News Brief
The USDA is planning to airdrop over 500,000 rabies vaccine baits across northwest Georgia, targeting raccoons and other wildlife, as part of an annual effort to stop the virus's spread. The operation, which involves helicopter and airplane drops starting in October, aims to reduce the human health and safety threat associated with rabies and eventually eliminate terrestrial rabies in the United States. Officials advise the public not to touch or move the baits, which are coated with fishmeal and labeled with a toll-free number for reporting. Residents should expect to see low-flying aircraft during the airdrop period and wash their hands if they come into contact with the vaccine. The program is a cooperative effort between the USDA, CDC, and local agencies.
Original source text (English)

Read Full Bio Dan Raby October 7, 2026 / 2:58 PM EDT / CBS Atlanta Add CBS News on Google The federal government is planning to airdrop more than 500,000 tiny packages that smell like fish across northwest Georgia in the next few weeks in an ongoing effort to protect the state's residents. The massive aerial operation is part of an annual operation to spread rabies vaccination baits targeting raccoons and other wildlife in the state. According to the USDA, more than 500,000 baits containing the RABORAL V-RG® oral rabies vaccine (ORV) will be spread across over 7,000 square kilometers in parts of Bartow, Catoosa, Chattooga, Dade, Floyd, Fannin, Gilmer, Gordon, Murray, Pickens, Rabun, Towns, Union, Walker, and Whitfield counties. The goal of the multi-state operation is to stop the spread of rabies westward and, along with other operations by the USDA, eventually eliminate terrestrial rabies from the United States. When will the rabies vaccine be airdropped? The Walker County government said that helicopter bait drops will begin on or about Oct. 7. Airplane drops will start around Oct. 20. Officials say residents in the affected counties should expect to spot low-flying airplanes or helicopters during these times. The program is a cooperative effort between the USDA, Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC), the Georgia Department of Public Health, and other local and federal agencies. It aims to reduce a significant human health and safety threat associated with rabies. Officials say the public should not try to touch or move the rabies vaccination bait. USDA What are rabies bait packets? The baits, packets coated with fishmeal crumbles, are designed to attract raccoons and other wildlife. Each bait is labeled with a toll-free information number, allowing residents to report any contact or ask questions. Officials advise the public not to touch or move the baits. If a bait is found in an open area, it can be carefully tossed deeper into the woods using gloves or a paper towel. People should wash their hands immediately if they come into contact with the vaccine inside a bait and contact their local health department for guidance. While the baits are not harmful to pets, eating several may cause mild stomach upset. Christopher Harris contributed to this report. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Rabies vaccine baits to be dropped across northwest Georgia cities - FOX 5 Atlanta

News Brief
Federal wildlife officials in northwest Georgia are distributing over 500,000 oral rabies vaccine baits via air drops to prevent the spread of raccoon rabies. The operation, which began on Wednesday, October 7, 2026, targets 15 counties and seven cities, covering more than 7,000 square kilometers. Helicopters will drop baits for two to three days, while low-flying airplanes will distribute them for about a week. The program is part of a nationwide effort to halt the westward movement of raccoon rabies, aiming to address a public health and safety emergency. Officials recommend leaving baits untouched so wild animals can access them and washing hands immediately after contact with the vaccine. The initiative is being carried out by the USDA’s Wildlife Services program in collaboration with the CDC and the Georgia Department of Health.

Helicopters begin dropping baits on Wednesday, Oct. 7, while low-flying airplanes will begin drops around Oct. 20. The fish-scented baits target raccoons across 15 Georgia counties and seven cities to protect public health. ATLANTA - Federal wildlife officials are distributing over 500,000 oral rabies vaccine baits via air drop across northwest Georgia to prevent the disease from spreading. What we know: The U.S. Department of Agriculture’s Wildlife Services program is dropping oral rabies vaccine baits across more than 7,000 square kilometers in northwest Georgia. Low-flying airplanes and helicopters are distributing the fish-scented baits to vaccinate raccoons and other wild animals. Helicopters begin baiting cities on Wednesday, Oct. 7, while airplane drops begin around Oct. 20. Helicopter drops will last two to three days, and airplane distribution will take about one week. The aerial baiting targets 15 counties: Bartow, Catoosa, Chattooga, Dade, Floyd, Fannin, Gilmer, Gordon, Murray, Pickens, Rabun, Towns, Union, Walker and Whitfield. Helicopters will specifically cover the cities of Dalton, Lafayette, Rome, Calhoun, Fort Oglethorpe, Chickamauga and Tunnel Hill. The program is part of a nationwide effort to halt the westward movement of raccoon rabies. Federal officials are conducting the operation to address a public health and safety emergency. What you can do: Officials advise leaving intact baits alone so wild animals can find them. If an intact bait is in the open, toss it deeper into the woods using gloves or a towel, then wash your hands immediately. Do not try to take a bait away from a pet, as animals may bite out of instinct. While the baits do not harm pets, eating many of them can cause an upset stomach. Anyone who touches the liquid vaccine inside a bait should wash their hands right away and call their local health department. Questions about the Wildlife Services Oral Rabies Vaccination Program can be directed to the USDA at 1-866-487-3297. The Source: The information in this story was gathered from the U.S. Department of Agriculture Wildlife Services program, which announced details of the oral rabies vaccination initiative alongside the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention and the Georgia Department of Health. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

空投 狂犬病 疫苗 如何防止病毒传播到诺克斯维尔

How air-dropping rabies vaccines keeps the virus from spreading into Knoxville
新闻简报田纳西州卫生部和美国农业部正在合作,在包括田纳西州在内的五个州分发口服 狂犬病 疫苗 (ORV) 诱饵,以防止 狂犬病 向西传播,特别是通过浣熊传播到诺克斯维尔。该诱饵分发计划自 2002 年以来一直在进行,旨在沿阿巴拉契亚山脉建立屏障,阻止浣熊 狂犬病 变种的传播。这些诱饵涂有鱼油和鱼粉碎屑以吸引浣熊,将于2026年10月7日至12日在城市和郊区以及2026年10月15日至25日在农村地区投放。该计划成功地阻止了疫情向西蔓延,没有病例穿过该地区到达诺克斯维尔。美国农业部强调,这些诱饵对人类和宠物来说是安全的,并提供了处理这些诱饵的指南,以避免潜在的接触。该举措是减少并可能消除该地区浣熊 狂犬病 的更广泛努力的一部分。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该计划目前正在实施中,城市和郊区从2026年10月7日开始投放,持续到2026年10月12日,农村地区从2026年10月15日至25日投放。
地点与对象
该计划针对田纳西州雅典、查尔斯顿、科利奇代尔、迪凯特、伊丽莎白顿、欧文、约翰逊城、山城和赖斯维尔的城市和郊区社区,以及布拉德利、卡特、格林、汉密尔顿、约翰逊、麦克明、梅格斯、波尔克、雷亚、沙利文、尤尼科伊和华盛顿县的农村地区。
事件
田纳西州卫生部和美国农业部正在合作分发口服 狂犬病 疫苗 (ORV) 诱饵,以防止 狂犬病(特别是浣熊)向西传播到诺克斯维尔。该计划成功地阻止了疫情向西蔓延,没有病例穿过该地区到达诺克斯维尔。
规模、影响与风险
ORV 诱饵分发计划旨在为尽可能多的浣熊接种疫苗,以减少并可能消除浣熊 狂犬病 变种。该计划自 2002 年以来一直在进行,是沿阿巴拉契亚山脉修建屏障的更大努力的一部分。虽然 狂犬病 在人类中很少见,但如果感染,可能会造成严重后果。
应对、状态与不确定性
该计划正在积极实施,利用直升机和飞机分发诱饵。美国农业部提供了有关处理诱饵的指南,并强调接触 疫苗 诱饵对人类无害。该计划被认为成功地阻止了 狂犬病 向西蔓延。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

How air-dropping rabies vaccines keeps the virus from spreading into Knoxville

News Brief
The Tennessee Department of Health and the U.S. Department of Agriculture are collaborating to distribute oral rabies vaccine (ORV) baits over five states, including Tennessee, to prevent the westward spread of rabies, particularly from raccoons, into Knoxville. The bait distribution program, which has been ongoing since 2002, aims to create a barrier along the Appalachian Mountains to stop the spread of the raccoon rabies variant. The baits are coated with fish oil and fishmeal crumbles to attract raccoons and are being dropped in urban and suburban areas from October 7 to 12, 2026, and in rural areas from October 15 to 25, 2026. The program has been successful in preventing the spread westward, with no cases making it through the zone to Knoxville. The USDA has emphasized that the baits are safe for humans and pets, and they have provided guidance on handling them to avoid potential exposure. The initiative is part of a broader effort to reduce and potentially eliminate raccoon rabies in the region.
Original source text (English)
KNOXVILLE, Tenn. (WATE) — The Tennessee Department of Health has joined forces with the U.S. Department of Agriculture to combat the spread of rabies by dropping oral rabies vaccine (ORV) baits over five states. The bait distribution program is part of an effort to prevent the spread of the rabies virus by wild animals, most often raccoons, to the west. The ORV bait is coated with fish oil and fishmeal crumbles, which give off a pungent odor and attract raccoons to them. When an animal bites into the bait, the vaccine coats the back of their throat and vaccinates them." RABORAL V-RG® coated sachet oral rabies vaccine baits distributed in Tennessee (Photo via USDA) "We're targeting raccoons because they seem to be an animal that people like to associate with or befriend, shall we say," said Betsy Haley, GIS planner and wildlife biologist for the National Rabies Management Program in USDA. "When [people] feed their animals outside, the raccoons come up, and they think it's cute. Well, by vaccinating the raccoons, we're helping to protect those pets, as well as those pet owners from potential rabies exposure." The USDA said a total of 2.4 million baits will be distributed in east Tennessee, western North Carolina, southwest Virginia, north central Alabama, and north Georgia. In Tennessee, over 200,000 baits will be dropped. Starting October 7 to 12, helicopters will drop baits in urban and suburban communities in Athens, Charleston, Collegedale, Decatur, Elizabethton, Erwin, Johnson City, Mountain City, and Riceville. Then, between October 15-25, low-flying airplanes will drop baits in rural areas in Bradley, Carter, Greene, Hamilton, Johnson, McMinn, Meigs, Polk, Rhea, Sullivan, Unicoi, and Washington counties. Don't approach elk: NPS warning follows injury in Great Smoky Mountains The ORV baits have been distributed in Tennessee since 2002 as part of a larger effort to prevent the westward spread of raccoon rabies by creating a barrier along the Appalachian Mountains from the Canadian border to Alabama. Haley said the program has been very successful in preventing the spread west, with no cases making it through the zone to Knoxville. "We've pushed the raccoon rabies cases back to the very eastern tip there in the north-northeast part of the state as well as some southern areas around Chattanooga and Cleveland area we're still baiting, but for the most part in Tennessee it's working very well," said Haley. Rabies infects the central nervous system, causing inflammation of the brain and eventually death. While the disease is rare in humans, with only 1 to 3 cases reported a year according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, it can have serious consequences. The majority of cases are found in wild animals, according to the CDC. "The hopes for these programs are to vaccinate as many raccoons as we can. These vaccine baits will work with other wildlife, but we are specifically targeting raccoons to try to reduce and potentially eliminate raccoon rabies variant from the environment," said Haley. Haley added that raccoons are the most common carrier of rabies in wildlife in the East. Because of this, the USDA asks people not to feed their animals outside and to keep their trash secured to limit contact with raccoons that could be carrying the virus. Could Tennessee try to execute Christa Pike again? Lawyer explains fallout, proposed legal changes The oral rabies vaccine bait packets will also be marked with a toll-free number (1-877-722-6725) for assistance or information if someone finds or comes in contact with a bait packet. "If you find the bait, don't freak out; don't automatically pick it up. If you need to move it, if it's in a spot where, say, it might have accidentally fallen on the sidewalk, or it's right on the edge of your yard, you can either use a paper towel or rubber gloves, pick it up and move it. And we say to do that just because the fish on the outside of the packet really does leave a pungent odor on your fingers," said Haley. Haley added that if a person or their pet comes into contact with the bait, it is not going to harm them. "It may, at worst, get an upset stomach from eating the bait, but don't try to take the bait out of the dog's mouth, because you could potentially expose yourself to the vaccine. And all that would do is potentially give you a rash. So, if you need to take it out of the dog's mouth, wash your hands really well with soap and water and keep an eye on that spot if you do get scratched," said Haley. View five news elements
Time
The program is currently being implemented, with bait drops starting on October 7, 2026, and continuing through October 12, 2026, for urban and suburban areas, and then from October 15 to 25, 2026, for rural areas.
Location and population
The program is targeting urban and suburban communities in Athens, Charleston, Collegedale, Decatur, Elizabethton, Erwin, Johnson City, Mountain City, and Riceville in Tennessee, as well as rural areas in Bradley, Carter, Greene, Hamilton, Johnson, McMinn, Meigs, Polk, Rhea, Sullivan, Unicoi, and Washington counties.
Event
The Tennessee Department of Health and the U.S. Department of Agriculture are collaborating to distribute oral rabies vaccine (ORV) baits to prevent the westward spread of rabies, particularly from raccoons, into Knoxville. The program has been successful in preventing the spread westward, with no cases making it through the zone to Knoxville.
Scale, impact and risk
The ORV bait distribution program aims to vaccinate as many raccoons as possible to reduce and potentially eliminate the raccoon rabies variant. The program has been ongoing since 2002 and is part of a larger effort to create a barrier along the Appalachian Mountains. While rabies is rare in humans, it can have serious consequences if contracted.
Response, status and uncertainty
The program is actively being implemented with the use of helicopters and airplanes to distribute baits. The USDA has provided guidance on handling the baits and has emphasized that contact with the vaccine baits is not harmful to humans. The program is considered successful in preventing the spread of rabies westward.

时间

阿什维尔和附近的三个山区城镇,包括本月所有三种方法都覆盖的邦科姆县。这些鱼饵分布在北卡罗来纳州西部 17 个县。
事件
直升机和飞机开始在阿什维尔及周边地区投放 狂犬病 疫苗 诱饵,目的是给浣熊接种疫苗。该事件由北卡罗来纳州卫生与公众服务部 (NCDHHS) 和美国农业部动植物卫生检验局报道。
规模、影响与风险
该计划旨在今年在北卡罗来纳州分发大约600,000个诱饵,作为美国农业部更大规模的倡议的一部分,该倡议计划在东部13个州分发约4.5万个诱饵。这些诱饵是为浣熊准备的,但存在儿童或宠物与其发生互动的风险。

Asheville-Area Families Should Keep Kids and Pets Away From Rabies Vaccine Baits as Helicopter and Airplane Drops Begin - Medical Daily

News Brief
Asheville-area families are advised to keep children and pets away from rabies vaccine baits being dropped by helicopters and airplanes starting on October 9, 2026. The North Carolina Department of Health and Human Services (NCDHHS) and the USDA's Animal and Plant Health Inspection Service are conducting this initiative to vaccinate raccoons. The baits are distributed in Buncombe County and 17 other western counties, with a total of 600,000 baits planned for North Carolina this year. The program, which has been ongoing since 2006, involves a partnership between federal and state agencies. While the baits are safe for people and pets if left undisturbed, there is a risk of accidental interaction. The NCDHHS recommends leaving baits where they are if found and checking outdoor spaces for them. The release does not provide specific flight paths or times, so residents are encouraged to remain vigilant.

Healthy Living Asheville-Area Families Should Keep Kids and Pets Away From Rabies Vaccine Baits as Helicopter and Airplane Drops Begin Published Oct 07, 2026 10:30 AM EDT By Dorothy Brooks Peter McCabe | AFP via Getty Images Share on Twitter Share on Facebook Share on Pocket Families in Asheville and three nearby mountain towns may soon find small fishmeal-scented packets in their yards, parks and driveways. Starting Friday, Oct. 9, helicopters will drop oral rabies vaccine baits over greater Asheville, Burnsville, Mars Hill and Waynesville through Oct. 13, according to a North Carolina Department of Health and Human Services news release issued Oct. 5. Low-flying airplanes will then cover 17 western counties from Oct. 15 to 22. The baits are meant for raccoons, but children pick up odd objects and dogs love anything fishy. State officials say an intact bait will not harm people, pets, or wildlife, yet they want households to know what to do if a child or dog gets to one first. One clarification matters: helicopters are used only over those four town areas. Crews began placing baits by hand around Asheville on Oct. 5 through Oct. 9, and the wider rural coverage comes from fixed-wing airplanes. Drop Schedule for Buncombe, Haywood, Yancey and 14 Other Counties The airplane phase, set for Oct. 15 through Oct. 22, covers Ashe, Avery, Buncombe, Cherokee, Clay, Graham, Haywood, Henderson, Jackson, Macon, Madison, McDowell, Mitchell, Swain, Transylvania, Watauga and Yancey counties, NCDHHS said. Because Asheville is in Buncombe County, that county is covered by all three methods this month. NCDHHS and the U.S. Department of Agriculture expect to distribute roughly 600,000 baits in North Carolina this year. The release does not list street-level flight paths or exact times, so residents of the listed areas should check yards and other outdoor spaces. The USDA's Animal and Plant Health Inspection Service announced in July that it planned to spread about 4.5 million baits in 2026 across 13 eastern states, from Maine to Alabama. North Carolina's program has run since 2006 as a partnership among USDA Wildlife Services and several state agencies, according to the state's oral rabies vaccine program page. Spotting a Bait Before a Child or Dog Does Most baits used in this program contain the Raboral V-RG vaccine. Per the USDA's oral rabies vaccine page, the vaccine sits in a plastic sachet that is either sprinkled with a fishmeal coating or sealed inside a hard fishmeal-polymer block about the size of a matchbox. The state program page likens it to a ketchup packet, in a white plastic package with pink liquid inside. The fishmeal smell draws raccoons to chew the packet open, and it can attract dogs too. If you or your pet come across a bait, NCDHHS says to leave it where it is. The exception is a bait on a lawn, driveway or other open spot unlikely to attract raccoons. In that case, the state says it can be moved to thicker cover where raccoons are more likely to find it, and pets are less likely to reach it. Wear gloves to pick it up. NCDHHS advises using gloves to throw a broken or ruptured bait in the regular trash. Wash hands thoroughly with soap and water if the packet may have broken open. A Swallowed Bait, a Curious Toddler and the Hotline Number If a child or an adult swallows a bait, the state release tells families to contact their local health department or call the phone number printed on the bait: 1-877-722-6725. Saving that number in a phone during the drop window can save time. NCDHHS says eating a bait won't harm a pet, though eating several could temporarily upset its stomach. The USDA says dogs that eat multiple baits face no long-term health risks. Neither people nor pets can catch rabies from contact with the baits, according to the USDA, and the state program page notes the vaccine contains only a single rabies virus gene, not live rabies virus. The real danger remains rabies itself. In 2025, the North Carolina State Laboratory of Public Health tested nearly 4,000 animals and about 6.5% came back positive, NCDHHS said. Raccoons had the most positive results, followed by skunks. Anyone bitten or scratched by a wild animal should wash the wound and promptly contact a health care professional or the local health department. NCDHHS notes that rabies is almost always fatal in mammals, including people, once symptoms develop. Pet Vaccines Still Do the Heavy Lifting Wildlife baiting is an added barrier, not a replacement for household protection. Dr. Carl Williams, NCDHHS public health veterinarian, said in the release that vaccinated pets remain the best prevention, and that vaccinating animals around the home "adds another layer of protection against the spread of this deadly disease." State law requires dogs, cats and ferrets to be vaccinated against rabies by 4 months of age and to stay current afterward. Families who are unsure whether a pet's shot has lapsed can check vaccination records with their veterinarian or ask the county health department whether any low-cost rabies clinics are scheduled. The larger goal reaches beyond the mountains. According to the state program page, the vaccine barrier runs from eastern Ohio down the Appalachian ridge to northeastern Alabama, with the goal of keeping raccoon rabies from spreading into Tennessee. MedicalDaily recently reported a different rabies risk in Southern California, where Los Angeles County's rabid bat count tied a record and health officials urged families finding bats indoors to call a doctor. For western North Carolina households, the next two weeks call for awareness rather than alarm. Look around before letting toddlers or dogs out, leave intact baits alone or move them with gloves, and keep the 1-877-722-6725 number handy until the airplane phase ends Oct. 22. Questions about a bite, scratch, or swallowed bait should go to a clinician or the local health department. Read more Rabid Raccoons Trigger 60-Day Alerts in Orange Park and Stuart as Pet Owners Are Urged to Update Shots Hinesville Fox Tests Positive for Rabies After Attacking Resident Near Bryant Commons Park, Prompting Pet Vaccine Reminder Rabid Cat Attacks Two People Near Jimmy Carter Boulevard in Norcross as Gwinnett Health Officials Urge Fast Treatment Key Questions Answered When and where are rabies vaccine baits being dropped in western North Carolina? Hand placement around Asheville runs Oct. 5 through Oct. 9. Helicopters cover greater Asheville, Burnsville, Mars Hill, and Waynesville from Oct. 9 to 13. Airplanes then cover 17 western counties from Oct. 15 to 22, according to NCDHHS. What do the baits look like? They are small plastic sachets coated in fishmeal or sealed inside hard fishmeal-polymer blocks about the size of a matchbox. The packet is often compared to a ketchup packet and lists a phone number for questions. What should I do if my child touches or swallows a bait? If the packet may have broken, wash your child's hands thoroughly with soap and water. If a bait is swallowed, NCDHHS says to contact your local health department or call the number on the bait, 1-877-722-6725. Is a bait dangerous if my dog eats it? State and federal officials say eating a bait won't harm a pet, though several baits could cause a temporary upset stomach. The USDA says pets and people cannot get rabies from the baits. Can I move a bait I find in my yard? Yes, if it sits on a lawn, driveway, or other open area. Use gloves and place it in a thicker cover where raccoons can reach it. Broken baits should be thrown in the regular trash, using gloves. Does this replace my pet's rabies shot? No. North Carolina law requires dogs, cats, and ferrets to be vaccinated by 4 months of age and kept current. The wildlife baits add protection but do not cover household pets. FG 寻求负责任的宠物主人 疫苗接种,以防止因狗引发的人类死亡 狂犬病 - 尼日利亚人民公报

FG seeks responsible pet ownership, vaccination to prevent human deaths from dog-triggered rabies - Peoples Gazette Nigeria
新闻简报尼日利亚政府通过联邦畜牧业发展部呼吁负责任的宠物饲养和 疫苗接种,以防止因狗引发的 狂犬病 造成人类死亡。这项举措是旨在到 2030 年消除此类死亡的更广泛宣传活动的一部分。2026 年 1 月至 8 月期间,该部在 16 个州记录了 100 多例动物 狂犬病 病例,强调了该疾病的 狂犬病。狂犬病 由狗唾液感染,会攻击中枢神经系统,如果不及时治疗可能致命。世界卫生组织已承诺支持尼日利亚的 狂犬病 调查和应对协调。尼日利亚疾病控制和预防中心建议动物咬伤受害者立即用肥皂和流水清洗伤口至少15分钟,并立即寻求紧急医疗护理。政府正在推广“同一个健康”方法,将人类、动物和环境卫生部门结合起来,以有效对抗 狂犬病。这项努力需要政府、卫生专业人员、社区和狗主人之间的持续合作,以降低风险并实现 2030 年目标。
查看新闻五要素
时间
此次宣传活动于 2026 年 10 月 7 日星期四开展,活动​​日期为 2026 年 1 月至 8 月,联邦畜牧业发展部在此期间记录了 100 多起动物 狂犬病 病例。
地点与对象
这个问题与尼日利亚全国有关,特别是在 16 个州,记录了 100 多起动物 狂犬病 病例。受影响人群包括狗主人和面临 狂犬病 传播 风险的社区。
事件
尼日利亚政府通过联邦畜牧业发展部呼吁负责任的宠物饲养和 疫苗接种,以防止因狗引发的 狂犬病 造成人类死亡。这是一项宣传活动的一部分,旨在到 2030 年结束由狗介导的 狂犬病 造成的人类死亡。
规模、影响与风险
联邦畜牧业发展部报告称,2026 年 1 月至 8 月期间,16 个州发生了 100 多例动物 狂犬病 病例。狂犬病 是从狗唾液中感染的,会攻击中枢神经系统,如果不及时处理,会对人类健康构成重大风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
联邦畜牧业发展部和世界卫生组织 (WHO) 正在通过调查和应对协调合作打击 狂犬病。尼日利亚疾病控制和预防中心建议动物咬伤受害者立即清洗伤口并寻求紧急医疗护理。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

FG seeks responsible pet ownership, vaccination to prevent human deaths from dog-triggered rabies - Peoples Gazette Nigeria

News Brief
The Nigerian government, through the Federal Ministry of Livestock Development, has called for responsible pet ownership and vaccination to prevent human deaths from dog-triggered rabies. This initiative is part of a broader sensitisation campaign aimed at ending such deaths by 2030. Between January and August 2026, the ministry recorded over 100 cases of animal rabies across 16 states, highlighting the disease's prevalence. Rabies, contracted from dog saliva, attacks the central nervous system and can be fatal if not treated promptly. The WHO has pledged support for rabies investigations and response coordination in Nigeria. The Nigeria Centre for Disease Control and Prevention has advised victims of animal bites to wash wounds immediately with soap and running water for at least 15 minutes and seek urgent healthcare without delay. The government is promoting a One Health approach, combining human, animal, and environmental health sectors, to combat rabies effectively. This effort requires sustained collaboration between government, health professionals, communities, and dog owners to reduce risks and achieve the 2030 goal.
Original source text (English)
Saturday, October 10, 2026 Menu #EndSARS Politics Anti-Corruption Rights Economy Education Health Sport Nationwide Opinion Search #EndSARS Politics Anti-Corruption Rights Economy Education Health Sport Nationwide Opinion Menu FG seeks responsible pet ownership, vaccination to prevent human deaths from dog-triggered rabies The statement noted that the WHO would support Nigeria in rabies investigations and response coordination. ADEFEMOLA AKINTADE • October 8, 2026 Rottweiler and Alsatian The Nigerian government has advocated responsible animal ownership, urging dog owners to ensure their pets are vaccinated from time-to-time. The call was made during a sensitisation campaign aimed at ending human deaths from dog-triggered rabies by 2030. Rabies is largely contracted from dog saliva when it makes contact with broken skin or injuries which attacks the body’s central nervous system. The Federal Ministry of Livestock Development said it recorded over a 100 cases of animal rabies across 16 states between January and August 2026, highlighting the rampancy of the disease in the nation. “Protecting communities from rabies requires everyone to play a part. We must stop working in silos and continue to educate families, encourage responsible animal ownership, and work together to reduce risks in our communities,” Chinyere Ijeoma Akujobi, permanent secretary, Federal Ministry of Livestock Development, said in a statement on Thursday. Nigeria has adopted One Health approach combining human, animal and environmental health sectors to combat rabies. One Health officer at the Federal Ministry of Health and Social Welfare, Dorcas Mernyi, called for prompt treatment in the event of exposure. She said, “No one should die from rabies simply because they did not know what to do after a dog bite or could not access treatment. We must ensure health workers recognise possible exposure immediately and that patients receive appropriate care without delay.” The WHO Emergency Preparedness and Response Cluster Lead in Nigeria, Alexander Chimbaru, says death prevention was team work requiring cooperation of all citizens. “Ending human deaths from dog-mediated rabies requires sustained collaboration between government, health and veterinary professionals, communities and dog owners. Every dog vaccinated, every exposure reported early and every person who receives timely care takes us closer to the 2030 goal,” Mr Chimbaru said. The statement noted that the World Health Organisation would support Nigeria in rabies investigations and response coordination. The Nigeria Centre for Disease Control and Prevention advised victims of animal bites and scratches to immediately wash the wound with soap and running water for a minimum of 15 minutes and urgently seek health care. The centre urged victims not to wait for symptoms to appear before seeking treatment. 从咬伤到致命疾病:狂犬病 威胁 - 肯尼亚星报

From bite to fatal disease: The rabies threat - The Star, Kenya
新闻简报肯尼亚《星报》于 2026 年 10 月 7 日发表的文章强调了 狂犬病 威胁,强调这是一种 疫苗 可预防的病毒性疾病,影响中枢神经系统,一旦出现症状几乎总是致命的。狂犬病 每年造成约 59,000 人死亡,主要发生在非洲和亚洲,其中儿童约占死亡人数的 40%。传播 的主要传播途径是被感染动物(尤其是狗)咬伤和抓伤,高达 99% 的病例都是由狗咬伤和抓伤。早期症状包括发烧、疼痛和伤口部位刺痛,逐渐发展为严重的大脑和脊髓炎症。文章强调了立即用肥皂和水清洗伤口至少 15 分钟并就医的重要性。它还指出,狂犬病 死亡可以通过大量狗 疫苗接种 和及时的 暴露后预防 来预防。内容为部分原创,并未提供具体暴发或案例的完整细节。

时间
从咬伤到致命疾病:狂犬病 威胁/HILLARY BETT 狂犬病 是一种 疫苗 可预防的病毒性疾病,会影响中枢神经系统,一旦出现临床症状,几乎总是致命的。
地点与对象
超过 150 个国家,其中约 95% 的死亡发生在非洲和亚洲。儿童约占人类 狂犬病 死亡人数的 40%。
事件
早期症状可能包括发烧、疼痛以及伤口部位异常刺痛或烧灼感。随着疾病的进展,它会引起大脑和脊髓的严重炎症。
规模、影响与风险
世界卫生组织估计,狂犬病 每年导致约 59,000 人死亡。高达 99% 的人类 狂犬病 病例是由狗引起的,该病毒主要通过受感染动物的咬伤和抓伤传播。通过大量狗的 狂犬病 和及时的 暴露后预防 可以预防 狂犬病 死亡。

被潜在感染动物咬伤或抓伤的人应立即用肥皂和水彻底清洗伤口至少 15 分钟,并就医。内容为部分原文,并未提供暴发或具体案例的完整细节。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

From bite to fatal disease: The rabies threat - The Star, Kenya

News Brief
The article from The Star, Kenya, published on October 7, 2026, highlights the rabies threat, emphasizing that it is a vaccine-preventable viral disease affecting the central nervous system and is almost always fatal once symptoms appear. Rabies causes approximately 59,000 human deaths annually, predominantly in Africa and Asia, with children accounting for about 40% of these deaths. The primary transmission route is through bites and scratches from infected animals, particularly dogs, which are responsible for up to 99% of cases. Early symptoms include fever, pain, and tingling at the wound site, progressing to severe brain and spinal cord inflammation. The article stresses the importance of immediate wound cleaning with soap and water for at least 15 minutes and seeking medical care. It also notes that rabies deaths are preventable through mass dog vaccination and timely post-exposure prophylaxis. The content is a partial original body and does not provide full details on specific outbreaks or cases.
Original source text (English)
From bite to fatal disease: The rabies threat/HILLARY BETT Rabies is a vaccine-preventable viral disease that affects the central nervous system and is almost always fatal once clinical symptoms appear. The World Health Organization (WHO) estimates that rabies causes about 59,000 human deaths each year in more than 150 countries, with about 95% of deaths occurring in Africa and Asia. Children account for approximately 40% of human rabies deaths. Dogs are responsible for up to 99% of human rabies cases, with the virus transmitted mainly through bites and scratches from infected animals. Early symptoms can include fever, pain and unusual tingling or burning at the wound site. As the disease progresses, it can cause severe inflammation of the brain and spinal cord. Rabies deaths are preventable through mass vaccination of dogs and timely post-exposure prophylaxis. People bitten or scratched by a potentially infected animal should immediately wash the wound thoroughly with soap and water for at least 15 minutes and seek medical care

叙利亚病例上升:蝙蝠是罪魁祸首吗? - 埃纳布巴拉迪 - 埃纳布巴拉迪

Rabies Cases Rise in Syria: Are Bats to Blame? - Enab Baladi - Enab Baladi
新闻简报Enab Baladi 于 2026 年 10 月 9 日发表的文章报告称,与 2025 年同期相比,2026 年第一季度叙利亚各省的 狂犬病 病例增加了 62.7%。病例的增加引发了关于蝙蝠在 传播 中的作用的争论,一些当地报道将这种疾病归因于蝙蝠袭击。虽然文章指出蝙蝠是 储存宿主 的天然 储存宿主,并且可以通过直接接触传播 储存宿主,即使没有明显的伤口,但它并没有证实蝙蝠是 暴发 的主要原因。暴发 影响了人类和牲畜,导致因扑杀和处置受感染动物而造成的经济损失。塔尔图斯大学微生物学教授 Fahem Abdalaziz 博士解释说,狂犬病 是一种严重的疾病,一旦出现症状,几乎 100% 致命,及时预防 狂犬病 至关重要。文章还强调了叙利亚存在多种蝙蝠,包括库氏蝙蝠和穴居蝙蝠,并强调避免接触蝙蝠以防止感染的重要性。蝙蝠在当前 暴发 中的作用仍在调查中,文章强调需要谨慎和预防措施。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026年第一季度,叙利亚狂犬病病例数量上升,文章发表于2026-10-09。
